# Sprint Evidence Ledger

**Last updated:** 2026-07-02 KST
**Purpose:** 스프린트 문서의 완료 체크박스가 실제 코드, 게이트, GitHub PR/merge commit과 서로 다르게 말하지 않도록 추적한다.

이 문서는 비개발자 관점의 “완료 영수증”이다. 스프린트 문서에서 `[x]`로 표시된 현재 상태 항목은 여기의 evidence id를 통해 PR, merge commit, 테스트, 품질 게이트 중 하나 이상과 연결되어야 한다.

## Checkbox Tracking Rules

- 상태 추적용 `[x]` 체크박스는 이 문서의 evidence id, GitHub PR, merge commit, 또는 검증 명령과 연결한다.
- 상태 추적용 `[ ]` 체크박스는 아직 완료로 보지 않는다. PR/merge evidence가 생기기 전까지는 계획 또는 남은 작업이다.
- `foundry_lite_python_engineering_guidelines_ko.md`의 코드 리뷰/장애 대응/금지 패턴 체크리스트처럼 “매 변경 때 사람이 확인하는 템플릿”은 제품 완료 상태가 아니다. 그런 체크박스는 일부러 `[ ]`로 남겨도 stale status가 아니다.
- 완료 체크박스를 바꾸는 PR은 이 문서도 같은 PR에서 갱신한다.

|
| `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S46`                        | `pnpm --silent quality:semantic-doc-consistency`; `pnpm --silent quality:data-pattern-matrix` | PASS. S46 static gates keep README/current-status/roadmap wording, active-covered infra references, deferred/partial data-pattern claims, proof levels, owners, reasons, and future tests synchronized. This is the evidence behind the S46 `[x]` status in the data-platform expansion docs. |
| `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S47-S48`                    | `DOCKER_HOST=unix:///Users/isihyeon/.colima/default/docker.sock TESTCONTAINERS_DOCKER_SOCKET_OVERRIDE=/var/run/docker.sock pnpm --silent quality:record-dlq-replay`; `pnpm --silent quality:late-data`; `pnpm --silent quality:watermark` | PASS. S47 covers the stream/source Record DLQ replay slice plus the Python transform row-error quarantine and snapshot transform-specific replay slice: bad CDC records are quarantined, valid records keep committing, replay is idempotent, bulk replay reports partial success with per-item `failedItems` and counts, replay/discard/bulk-item-failure evidence is visible through Operations/API/SDK/UI paths, `Input.map_rows(..., on_error="quarantine")` quarantines row-local Python transform errors while committing clean mapped rows with transform-execution DLQ metadata and audit evidence, and `retry-transform` reruns the current snapshot transform with latest inputs, resolves the origin DLQ on clean output, and fails closed without output commit when row quarantine repeats or mode is append/incremental. The local S47 gate passed as `37 passed, 152 deselected` with the Colima Docker socket exported for Testcontainers. S48 is covered for the stream/source late-data watermark slice plus snapshot transform output and materialization boundary watermark propagation: event/source/process times, named timezone override, too-late Record DLQ, monotonic partition/source watermark, normalized `platformWatermark`, idempotent duplicate late delivery, reprocessing/materialization reopen evidence, late-data badges, downstream impact, stale late-delete guard, transform output `platformWatermark` derived from input dataset transaction watermarks with a min-input event-time policy and materialization output `platformWatermark` for action/object boundaries are fixed by focused gates. The local late-data and watermark gates passed as `3 passed, 38 deselected` and `16 passed, 40 deselected`. Append/incremental transform DLQ replay and full platform-wide watermark semantics remain future scope. |
| `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S49`                        | `pnpm --silent quality:multi-file-dataset`; `pnpm --silent quality:partition-pruning`; `pnpm --silent quality:s3-storage`; `pnpm --silent quality:iceberg` | PASS. `quality:multi-file-dataset` passed as `22 passed`. `quality:partition-pruning` passed as `8 passed, 18 deselected`. `quality:s3-storage` passed as `33 passed`; `quality:iceberg` passed as `24 passed, 20 warnings`. The S3 and Iceberg storage ratchets cover `test_s3_data_file_paths_apply_partition_filter_before_download`, `test_s3_commit_staged_files_retries_without_duplicate_parts`, `test_iceberg_commit_staged_files_retries_as_one_snapshot`, `test_iceberg_data_file_paths_skip_snapshot_when_partition_filter_has_no_match`, `test_iceberg_data_file_paths_prune_files_by_column_stats_without_full_materialization`, and `test_iceberg_commit_staged_files_replaces_head_without_clobbering_pinned_version`. This S49 slice proves storage adapters resolve manifest-listed data files in order, public `foundry.datasets.preview(...)` reads multi-file manifests through the same facade, unlisted files in the storage directory are not discovered by listing, dataset rows can declare layout policy fields, readable Parquet commit paths record manifest column min/max/null_count and sort_bounds evidence, local/fake storage commits multiple staged parquet parts into one deterministic manifest/version/DB file-row set, validation-after-tamper blocks that commit, stale retry orphan parts are not retained, high-cardinality partition layouts persist `partitionLayoutWarnings` in committed transaction metadata, S3 retries multi-part staged-file commits without duplicate parts, Iceberg commits multiple staged parts into one pinned snapshot with multiple data files while preserving older pinned versions, local/fake/S3 read/preview partition filters reduce actual manifest file reads, Iceberg skips reads when no manifest file matches, Iceberg column-stat filters download only matching snapshot data files without full materialization, and `test_sql_transform_pushes_partition_predicate_to_storage_adapter` proves a single-input SQL transform equality predicate reaches storage `partition_filter` and reduces the compute input file list. |
| `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S50`                        | `DOCKER_HOST=unix:///Users/isihyeon/.colima/default/docker.sock TESTCONTAINERS_DOCKER_SOCKET_OVERRIDE=/var/run/docker.sock pnpm --silent quality:iceberg-maintenance` | PASS. `quality:iceberg-maintenance` passed as `10 passed, 73 deselected, 4 warnings`. This S50 slice proves Operations can create an Iceberg maintenance dry-run plan, DB committed version snapshots are marked protected and kept out of deletion candidates, compaction candidates are reported from small-file/file-count/read-amplification policy, missing Iceberg tables return a no-op `no_table` plan, healthy tables with no matching policy trigger return `healthy`, SDK/API surfaces stay generated, and the plan request leaves audit evidence. It also proves `run_iceberg_maintenance`/`POST /api/operations/maintenance/iceberg/{dataset}/run`/`client.operations.icebergMaintenance.run(...)` can compact the current snapshot, preserve logical row hash, expire deletable orphan snapshots, clean no-longer-reachable data/sidecar files, keep DB committed manifests readable, and write run audit evidence. Transform/materialization/backup pin retention, concurrent maintenance fencing, fault-injected rollback proof, and ambiguous retry idempotency remain future scope. |
| `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S51`                        | `pnpm --silent quality:cdc-continuous-worker`; `pnpm --silent quality:kafka-rebalance` | PASS. `quality:cdc-continuous-worker` passed as `15 passed, 20 deselected`, and `quality:kafka-rebalance` passed as `1 passed, 29 deselected`. This S51 slice proves the stream archive worker has a bounded continuous mode that archives multiple committed batches through the existing transaction/checkpoint boundary, follows committed cursor state without dropping or duplicating archived event ids across loop iterations, stops on max-batch and empty-poll bounds, honors a stop callback before starting another batch, parses env/CLI continuous-loop controls, returns `CONFIGURATION_ERROR` JSON payloads for env/schema/tenant configuration failures, records workflow-run owner/token/expiry lease evidence, releases the lease with stop evidence, stops with `lease_lost` before another batch after a newer worker takes the lease, and stops a revoked partition assignment with `assignment_revoked` before committing the already-read batch. The same gate now proves `worker:cdc-object-indexer` can index committed CDC dataset versions in order, persist `lastIndexedVersionId`/`lastIndexedVersionNumber`/`lastIndexRunId` in `workflow_runs.output.cdcObjectIndexer.cursor`, resume after the last indexed version, run bounded/continuous loops until empty poll or lease loss, and fail closed when a CDC source version exceeds the configured row limit instead of partially indexing and advancing the cursor. [PR #18](https://github.com/ludia8888/foundry-lite/pull/18) hardens the active index version pointer, and [PR #19](https://github.com/ludia8888/foundry-lite/pull/19) upgrades promotion to compare-and-swap semantics. The repository contract proves fake, SQLite, and PostgreSQL can switch an object type to a new active index version even when there are no object rows to flip; stale switches fail if another promotion already changed the pointer; PostgreSQL allows only one winner when two connections concurrently create the first pointer for the same tenant/object type; and unsupported SQL dialects fail closed until they add their own native upsert contract. The integration proof creates an empty snapshot-backed object type, runs `index_shadow_rebuild`, then applies a CDC create event and verifies the inserted object row uses the shadow `indexVersion` recorded in `object_index_versions`. Schema revision `infra/schema_revisions/20260614_0002_object_index_active_pointer.json` freezes the active-pointer table. | `VERIFY-S3-STORAGE-RATCHET`                        | `DOCKER_HOST=unix:///Users/isihyeon/.colima/default/docker.sock TESTCONTAINERS_DOCKER_SOCKET_OVERRIDE=/var/run/docker.sock pnpm --silent quality:s3-storage`; `DOCKER_HOST=unix:///Users/isihyeon/.colima/default/docker.sock TESTCONTAINERS_DOCKER_SOCKET_OVERRIDE=/var/run/docker.sock uv run python scripts/quality/check_flaky_detector.py --iterations 3 --output artifacts/quality/flaky_s3_storage_ratchet.json --command 'uv run pytest tests/integration/test_s3_dataset_storage_adapter.py -q'`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | PASS. `32 passed` in the latest focused suite; an earlier focused flaky detector repeated the pre-footer S3 suite 3 times with stable `28 passed`. The first infrastructure ratchet adds `S3DatasetStorageAdapter` behind the existing `DatasetStorageAdapter` port and proves it against MinIO/Testcontainers. The suite covers `test_s3_dataset_storage_adapter_contract`, partial multipart timeout cleanup, unreadable parquet footer rejection/cleanup, storage-success/DB-failure orphan cleanup evidence, committed manifest missing detection, abort cleanup preserving an earlier committed manifest, concurrent version-prefix writes, retry-after-timeout idempotency for the same version id, and Operations `adapterFailure` evidence for S3 storage failures. On 2026-06-16 an adversarial re-verification found that several originally-claimed failure modes were simulated in ways that gave false confidence, and added seven hardenings with direct proofs: (1) `test_s3_real_multipart_upload_failure_aborts_orphaned_parts` starts a real multipart upload and proves `S3DatasetStorageAdapter._abort_multipart_uploads` removes parts that never appear in `list_objects_v2`; (2) `test_s3_read_path_detects_corrupted_data_object` and `test_s3_read_path_detects_truncated_data_object` prove `S3DatasetStorageAdapter._verify_local_copy` re-checks byte size and content hash on every download via `_download_manifest_file`, not only at commit; (3) `test_s3_transient_load_error_is_retryable_not_corruption`, `test_s3_transient_download_error_is_retryable_not_corruption`, and `test_s3_missing_data_object_on_read_is_reported_missing` prove `_is_not_found_error` routes only genuine missing objects to `FileNotFoundError` (storage-corrupt) while transient timeouts become a retryable `AdapterError`; (4) `test_s3_failed_commit_cleanup_failure_preserves_original_error_with_evidence` proves `S3DatasetStorageAdapter._safe_cleanup_failed_commit` preserves the original failure and records `orphan_cleanup` evidence when cleanup itself fails; (5) `test_s3_failed_commit_cleanup_uses_known_keys_not_listing` proves failed-commit cleanup deletes by known key so list-after-write lag cannot hide an orphan; (6) `test_s3_list_keys_paginates_beyond_single_response` proves `_list_keys` follows `ContinuationToken`; (7) `test_s3_duplicate_version_commit_is_rejected_without_destroying_existing` and `test_s3_transient_head_during_commit_guard_is_retryable` prove `S3DatasetStorageAdapter._guard_version_not_committed` rejects reusing a committed version id with a non-retryable `conflict` without destroying the existing commit, and `test_s3_adapter_error_during_verify_still_runs_cleanup` proves a non-conflict `AdapterError` mid-commit still cleans up. The `failure_contract` now also declares `load_manifest/unavailable` (retryable), `commit_staged_file/conflict`, and `commit_staged_file/validation` (non-retryable). Two end-to-end tests prove the product itself (not just the adapter in isolation) uses S3 when configured: `test_s3_composition_root_selects_adapter_from_profile_and_runs_full_cycle` builds dependencies through `create_local_core_dependencies(adapter_profile="s3-storage")` — exercising the `local_runtime` s3-storage selection branch, `_s3_storage_config` env wiring, and the real boto3 client construction — and runs ensure/upload/preview against MinIO; `test_s3_api_end_to_end_preview_reads_through_s3_and_surfaces_corruption` drives the FastAPI entrypoint over the S3-backed composition root, reads dataset versions/preview over HTTP, and proves a deleted committed manifest surfaces as a `committed_version_storage_missing` HTTP 400. A further ratchet pass threads the hardened behaviors through the **core engine** (not only the adapter boundary): `test_s3_real_multipart_interrupt_during_upload_is_visible_in_operations` (real multipart abort surfaces as a FAILED Operations run with a retryable `commit_staged_file` adapterFailure and no orphaned parts), `test_s3_corrupted_data_object_surfaces_through_engine_as_storage_corruption` (a tampered committed object surfaces as `committed_version_storage_corrupt` via `datasets.preview`), and `test_s3_transient_read_during_inspect_surfaces_retryable_not_corruption` (a timed-out read via `datasets.inspect` stays retryable, not corruption). Storage-checklist tricky items are closed one by one: row_count truthfulness vs the actual parquet (`test_s3_committed_manifest_row_count_matches_actual_parquet_rows`, A2), S3 parquet footer readability plus manifest row-count validation (`test_s3_commit_rejects_unreadable_parquet_footer_and_cleans_up`, `test_s3_commit_rejects_parquet_row_count_mismatch_and_cleans_up`, T0-011), per-transaction staging isolation so cleanup never deletes another retry attempt (`test_s3_staging_cleanup_is_isolated_per_transaction`, A5/A6), and 403/expired reads staying retryable rather than corruption (`test_s3_access_expiry_on_read_is_retryable_not_corruption`, A7). A4 (overwrite-impossible) is covered by the duplicate-version guard and A9 (APPEND concurrent ordering) by the dataset-lock version allocation; CSV/check policies (A3, A13-A15) remain scoped to the compute/check layer. |

## Commit-Point Risk Tracking

The detailed Tier 0 audit lives in [Commit-Point Risk Register](./commit-point-risk-register.md).
This ledger keeps the short, evidence-oriented view so sprint checkboxes do not
claim more than the code and gates currently prove.

| Stream and connector cursor commit                                | T0-01, T0-02                      | `S37-A7`, `S37-A9`, `S38-A1`-`S38-A4`, `VERIFY-REST-WEBHOOK-OPS`, `VERIFY-REST-WEBHOOK-TRICKY-EDGES`, `VERIFY-KAFKA-LIVE-BROKER`, `VERIFY-STREAM-REST-CURSOR-COMMIT-POINTS`, `test_kafka_live_broker_parallel_postgres_workers_do_not_duplicate_committed_offset`                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Partial. Normal committed cursor paths exist, local failure-injection proves stream offset / REST cursor do not advance when the next dataset write fails, REST page-number pagination is rejected as non-replayable, and live Kafka + PostgreSQL parallel workers cannot both commit the same offset because commit rechecks the stream cursor under the dataset lock. Remaining edge proofs are Kafka group rebalance revoke fencing and broker commit-unknown reconciliation around stream batches.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Dataset transaction, storage manifest, and candidate health check | T0-03, T0-04, T0-05, T0-06        | `S05-A1`, `S05-A2`, `S05-A3`, `S05-A5`, `S36A-A2`, `S36A-A3`, `VERIFY-DATASET-STORAGE-SPLIT-BRAIN`, `VERIFY-DATASET-VERSION-CONCURRENCY`, `VERIFY-DATASET-HEALTH-CANDIDATE`, `VERIFY-SCHEMA-COMPATIBILITY-TOCTOU`, `VERIFY-S3-STORAGE-RATCHET`, `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S54`                                                                                                                                          | Partial. Local MVP split-brain and version-race edges now have direct proofs: PostgreSQL concurrent commits allocate strictly increasing dataset version numbers under the dataset lock, duplicate version insertion maps to a domain conflict, promoted artifact cleanup runs after metadata conflict, FAILED run evidence is retained, missing manifest/data-file diagnosis works, manifest read verification works, abort cleanup does not delete committed artifacts, health checks inspect the candidate rather than latest, S54 quality check results persist the checked candidate fingerprint plus schema reference, a candidate changed after quality checks is rejected before final storage commit, row-level quarantine revalidates the cleaned candidate before commit, and schema validation runs under the dataset lock. For the S3-compatible profile, `VERIFY-S3-STORAGE-RATCHET` now proves multipart-interruption abort of orphaned parts, byte-size/content-hash verification on every read (not only at commit), parquet footer readability plus manifest row-count verification on commit/read, transient-vs-missing read classification, known-key cleanup that survives list-after-write lag, and a duplicate-version-id commit guard that does not destroy the existing commit. The composition-root selection path is now proven end to end (not just by injecting the adapter): `create_local_core_dependencies(adapter_profile="s3-storage")` selects the S3 adapter from `_s3_storage_config` env wiring, and the FastAPI entrypoint reads dataset versions/preview over HTTP through the S3-backed product and surfaces a deleted manifest as an HTTP 400 storage-corruption error. Remaining production storage gap is a genuine concurrent same-version-id isolation race (the upstream version allocator is the real uniqueness guarantee; the head-object guard is a backstop) and AWS S3 validation beyond MinIO. |
| CSV primary-key normalization                                     | T0-32                             | `VERIFY-CSV-PK-STRING-PRESERVATION`; `tests/contracts/test_compute_adapter_contract.py::test_csv_primary_key_preserves_leading_zeroes`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Covered for the current CSV-to-Parquet compute boundary. Primary-key values with leading zeroes remain strings, and schema inspection keeps the key column non-null string.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Transform version pinning and lineage                             | T0-07, T0-08, T0-33               | `VERIFY-FULL-CI-GATE`, `VERIFY-TRANSFORM-INPUT-PINNING`, `VERIFY-MATERIALIZED-TRANSFORM-PINNING`, `VERIFY-TRANSFORM-RETRY-NO-DUPLICATE-OUTPUT`, `VERIFY-TRANSFORM-LINEAGE-ATOMIC`, `VERIFY-TRANSFORM-OOM-STAGING-CLEANUP`, `VERIFY-SQL-TRANSFORM-FILESYSTEM-GUARD`, `VERIFY-PYTHON-TRANSFORM-SDK-HANDLES`, `VERIFY-TRANSFORM-SCHEDULER`, `quality:transform-scheduler`, `test_transform_retry_uses_failed_run_definition_snapshot`, OpenLineage P8, failed-transform retry evidence                              | Partial. Input version pinning is covered even when latest changes before SQL execution, including the materialized `ops.order_current` downstream path where `materialization.completed` carries the exact `versionId` and a newer materialization becomes latest before SQL execution. Transform runs now persist a definition snapshot so failed-run retry replays the original SQL template/check/output definition instead of the latest mutable transform row. Successful transform runs cannot be retried into duplicate outputs through the Operations retry path, lineage-write failure after output storage promotion now leaves no committed output version or lineage edge while cleaning the promoted artifact, compute failure after partial staging deletes the staged output, SQL transforms cannot directly read arbitrary filesystem paths. Python transforms now execute through SDK input/output handles without handing committed storage paths to user functions, and transform append mode commits output through APPEND dataset transactions (`test_python_transform_runs_through_sdk_handles_without_raw_storage_path`, `test_transform_append_output_mode_commits_append_transaction`). `run_graph` adds bounded downstream trigger evidence (`test_transform_graph_runs_downstream_transform_and_records_relation`), and the transform scheduler tick adds snapshot rebuild evidence when input dataset versions change (`test_transform_scheduler_tick_rebuilds_snapshot_when_input_version_changes`), skips append/incremental automatic tick fail-closed (`test_transform_scheduler_skips_append_mode_until_merge_policy_exists`), skips snapshot transforms with missing input versions without failing the whole scheduler (`test_transform_scheduler_skips_snapshot_until_input_version_exists`), and now has a bounded/continuous `worker:transform-scheduler` CLI/env/evidence proof (`tests/unit/test_transform_scheduler_worker.py`). Remaining transform hardening is full process sandbox/worker isolation, production lease/fencing/Kubernetes scheduler packaging, managed Temporal transform scheduling, append/incremental scheduler merge policy, and crash/process-kill proof after a successful output commit and before worker acknowledgement.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Action idempotency and atomic mutation                            | T0-09, T0-10, T0-11, T0-12, T0-13, T0-14, T0-34 | `S25-A1`-`S25-A5`, `S26-A1`-`S26-A3`, `S26-A5`, `S36A-A1`, `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S53`, `VERIFY-ACTION-IDEMPOTENCY-FINGERPRINT`, `VERIFY-ACTION-COMMIT-ATOMICITY`, `VERIFY-ACTION-AUDIT-MASKING`, `quality:action-writeback-live`, transaction outbox/audit pair gate, MVP data-correctness gate, SDK generated helpers, `test_action_expected_object_version_required`, `test_action_precondition_stale_read_conflicts_on_commit`                    | Partial. Same-key race, same-key/different-request fingerprint conflict, expected-object-version enforcement, current internal DB action commit atomicity, sensitive action audit masking, and real S3/MinIO external-writeback timeout/compensation/reconciliation invariants are covered. Remaining action hardening is managed unresolved-writeback queue/daemon/approval operations and later ERP-specific connector packaging, not the core timeout or landed-write/local-failure invariant.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Materialization watermarks                                        | T0-15, T0-16, T0-17               | `VERIFY-MATERIALIZATION-WATERMARKS`; `VERIFY-MATERIALIZATION-COMMIT-FAILURE`; `VERIFY-ACTION-LOG-SUCCESS-ONLY`; `VERIFY-ACTION-LOG-RERUN-NO-DUPLICATES`; `VERIFY-MATERIALIZATION-RETRY-NO-DUPLICATE-OUTPUT`; `S30-A1`-`S30-A4`; `S31-A1`-`S31-A3`; `S31-A5`                                                                                                                             | Covered for the current MVP materialization path. Late-committed actions and mid-run object edits are protected by completed-at/id cursors, tenant-scoped object change sequences, active-index-version pinning, run-start row capture, and append-only `object_record_versions` replay. Materialization output evidence now includes both the run watermark and a `materializes_to` lineage edge tied to the materialization run id and target dataset version id. Failed action runs stay available as operational evidence but are excluded from successful `ops.action_log` materialization rows, same-cursor reruns create fresh non-duplicated snapshots, metadata/outbox/lineage failure does not expose a downstream completion event before commit, and same-cursor retry after that failure commits exactly one output version. Failed `action_log` materialization still aborts before any output dataset version is visible with a `FAILED` run and no target version id.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| CDC ordering and tombstones                                       | T0-18, T0-19, T0-20               | `S40-A2`, `S40-A4`, `VERIFY-CDC-OBJECT-INDEXING`, `VERIFY-CDC-STALE-TOMBSTONE`, `VERIFY-CDC-PK-UPDATE-POLICY`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Covered for the current MVP CDC object-indexing path. Late stale update, late snapshot/read event, tombstone delete, late update-after-delete skip, query/materialization tombstone exclusion consistency, and primary-key update fail-closed policy are covered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Shadow reindex and search projection                              | T0-21, T0-22, T0-23               | `S41-A5`, `S41-H1`, `S41-H2`, `S42-A3`, `VERIFY-ACTIVE-INDEX-POINTER`, `VERIFY-ACTIVE-INDEX-POINTER-FLAKE`, `VERIFY-OBJECT-QUERY-CURSOR-GUARDS`, `VERIFY-SEARCH-INDEXING`, `VERIFY-SHADOW-SEARCH-PROJECTION-EDGES`                                                                                                                                                                      | Covered for the current T0 projection scope. Action-edit replay, active pointer safety, concurrent first-pointer stability, signed/query-shape cursor binding, active-index-version cursor fail-safe behavior, stale search document version no-op handling, and search-hit object-store refetch with projection/source version visibility are covered. Fallback degraded behavior remains a lower-tier backlog item, not a T0 source-of-truth blocker.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Security commit points                                            | T0-24, T0-25, T0-26, T0-31, T0-34 | `PR-43`, `S34-A3`, `S34-A4`, `S36A-A9`, `S53-A4`, `VERIFY-PRODUCTION-AUTH-GUARD`, `VERIFY-WORKER-TENANT-CONTEXT`, `VERIFY-LINK-TENANT-BOUNDARY`, `VERIFY-STATIC-OBJECT-SET-PERMISSION`, `VERIFY-STATIC` tenant/auth gates, `VERIFY-REST-WEBHOOK-TRICKY-EDGES`, `VERIFY-ACTION-AUDIT-MASKING`, `test_policy_classification.py`, `test_masked_property_cannot_filter_sort_search`, `test_dataset_preview_masks_sensitive_columns_for_unprivileged_roles`, `test_explain_does_not_bypass_property_masking`, `test_operations_read_is_operator_only_and_redacts_sensitive_params`, `test_sensitive_writeback_payload_is_masked_in_audit`, `test_rls_tenant_context_reset_between_pooled_connections` | Partial. Production header-trust fail-fast, worker tenant-context validation, link traversal tenant scoping, static object-set member permission/masking rechecks, REST private-network/redirect/DNS-rebinding SSRF guardrails, ontology-classified sensitive property/column masking across object/dataset/search/explain/Operations surfaces, action audit masking, sensitive writeback reconciliation audit masking, operator-only Operations read, and pooled PostgreSQL RLS tenant-context reset are covered. Remaining future backlog: aggregate/export/materialized-dataset masking when those surfaces exist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Backup, restore, and external replay                              | T0-09, T0-10, T0-27, T0-28        | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S53`, `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S53-APPROVAL-RELEASE`, `quality:action-writeback-live`, and S57 backup/restore preflight/restore-mode gates                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Partial. T0-09/T0-10 are covered for the current real S3/MinIO external-writeback adapter: timeout becomes `outcome_unknown`, landed write plus local failure becomes `compensation_required`, real `remote_lookup` reconciliation applies the local mutation once, and sensitive/high-risk writeback recovery can be released through backend/API/SDK approval id/reason evidence without exposing raw parameters. S57 covers backup/restore preflight, restore-mode retry lockout, write lockout, post-restore validation, and resume approval. Remaining future work is production backup artifact restore/executor packaging plus external-writeback managed review queue, managed daemon, visual approval UI, and ERP/webhook-specific connector packaging.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

| `S58A-A2` | JWT/OIDC AuthProvider validates human and service-account bearer identity before API context creation | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58A`; `JwtOidcAuthProvider`; `AUTHORIZATION_HEADER`; `jwt_oidc_auth_provider_from_env`; `FOUNDRY_LITE_OIDC_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_CLAIM`; `FOUNDRY_LITE_OIDC_REVOKED_JTIS_JSON`; `test_jwt_auth_provider_maps_verified_token_to_principal`; `test_expired_or_wrong_audience_jwt_is_denied`; `test_jwt_auth_provider_requires_time_and_identity_claims`; `test_jwks_rotation_keeps_valid_sessions`; `test_oidc_profile_loads_discovery_and_jwks_from_env`; `test_service_account_is_tenant_scoped`; `test_service_account_claim_is_configurable_from_env`; `test_session_revocation_policy_denies_revoked_jti`; `test_revocation_list_loads_from_env`; `test_production_strict_auth_profile_builds_jwt_oidc_provider` | Partial. The strict `jwt`/`oidc` profile now verifies local discovery/JWKS-backed RS256 bearer tokens, denies expired or wrong-audience tokens, requires `exp`, `iat`, `iss`, and `aud` claims, maps tenant/user/roles claims into `Principal`, maps M2M tokens without `sub` to `service-account:*` actors only when the token carries a tenant claim, ignores header tenant override in that strict path, supports configurable service-account claim names, blocks locally revoked JWT ids, and refreshes JWKS on unknown `kid` while keeping cached keys valid during rotation. Live discovery fetch, JWKS polling/TTL/key retirement, IdP introspection/refresh-token revocation, service-account registry/scope policy, and IdP group-to-role policy remain future work. |
| `S58A-A3` | REST connector credential secretRefs refresh between long-running snapshot calls | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58A`; `RestAuthConfig.token_secret_ref`; `RestAuthConfig.header_value_secret_ref`; `RestPullConnectorAdapter`; `SecretProvider`; `test_connector_refreshes_rotated_secret`; `test_rest_connector_secret_ref_requires_provider_without_leaking_value` | Partial. REST connector auth config can now carry a secretRef instead of a raw bearer/header credential, and the adapter resolves that secret through `SecretProvider` on every `snapshot()` call. The focused test proves a rotated local env secret is picked up by the next snapshot using the same adapter/config, and missing-provider errors redact the secret value. Full connector workflow data-plane execution, cloud/Vault manager watch or polling, refresh-token exchange, previous/current dual-read retry for providers that accept old and new credentials during rotation, and persistent rotation registry remain future work. |
| `S58B-A1` | Deterministic pseudonymization is stable within a tenant and separated across tenants | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `PrivacyTransformPlan`; `PrivacyFieldRule`; `transform_privacy_rows`; `test_same_identifier_maps_to_same_pseudonym_within_scope`; `test_pseudonym_differs_across_tenants` | Partial. The privacy transform can turn a configured identifier field into a deterministic HMAC pseudonym so the same tenant/scope/value maps to the same pseudonym on replay, while the same value in a different tenant maps differently and cannot be joined by raw pseudonym equality. Ontology-driven policy registry and environment replication job integration remain future work. |
| `S58B-A2` | Basic anonymization and text PII redaction remove raw PII samples from transformed rows | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `ANONYMIZED_VALUE`; `REDACTED_PII_VALUE`; `redact_text_pii`; `test_anonymized_dataset_contains_no_raw_pii_samples` | Partial. Configured fields can be replaced with `***ANONYMIZED***`, and local regex redaction removes email, SSN, and US phone samples from text fields before rows are emitted. This is not a full multilingual or ML-backed PII detector and does not yet include false-positive review or type-specific anonymization strategies. |
| `S58B-A3` | Privacy transform output carries replay-stable plan lineage metadata | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `PrivacyTransformResult`; `PrivacyTransformPlan.version`; `test_privacy_transform_is_versioned_and_replayable` | Partial. The transform result carries `privacyTransform` lineage with plan name, deterministic plan version, rule count, and row count, and the same input/tenant/plan replays to the same output. Durable runtime lineage persistence, approval workflow, and downstream environment replication workflow remain future work. |
| `S58B-A4` | Reversible mapping is kept outside transformed rows and operator evidence | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `InMemoryProtectedPrivacyMappingStore`; `PrivacyMappingRecord`; `test_reversible_mapping_requires_protected_store`; `test_reversible_mapping_is_stored_outside_rows_and_lineage` | Partial. A reversible pseudonymization rule now fails without an explicit protected mapping store, and the in-memory protected store can keep pseudonym-to-original mapping separately while transformed rows, lineage, and redacted evidence omit the raw original value. Durable/encrypted protected mapping backend, access-control audit, approval workflow, and environment replication integration remain future work. |
| `S58B-A5` | Production-to-nonprod replication requires a privacy plan for sensitive fields | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `PrivacyReplicationPolicy`; `validate_privacy_replication_policy`; `test_production_replication_requires_privacy_plan_for_sensitive_fields`; `test_replication_policy_rejects_unprotected_sensitive_fields`; `test_allowed_replication_policy_adds_lineage_without_raw_values` | Partial. A production source targeting staging, analytics, or AI experiment environments now fails closed when no privacy plan exists, or when a configured sensitive field is missing from the plan or marked `passthrough`. Allowed transforms attach replication policy evidence to lineage with source/target environment, reason, unprotected fields, and plan version, without carrying raw values. Durable environment replication workflow, tenant/environment policy registry, approval workflow, and runtime OpenLineage transport integration remain future work. |
| `S58B-A6` | Anonymized dataset lineage links source and target dataset versions without raw PII | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58B`; `PrivacyDatasetRef`; `build_privacy_openlineage_event`; `test_anonymized_dataset_lineage_links_source_and_target_versions`; `test_privacy_openlineage_event_excludes_raw_pii`; `test_privacy_openlineage_event_is_replay_stable` | Partial. Privacy transform lineage can now record source and anonymized target dataset version references, and the helper emits a replay-stable OpenLineage-compatible event artifact that carries plan/version/environment evidence without raw email, SSN, phone, or original mapping values. Runtime DB `lineage_edges`, outbox publication, OpenLineage backend/CLI transport, and durable environment replication workflow remain future work. |
| `S58C-A1` | Erasure requests expose HMAC subject evidence and tenant-scoped subject resolution | `VERIFY-DATA-PLATFORM-S58C`; `ErasureRequest`; `resolve_erasure_subject`; `test_erasure_subject_hash_is_secret_hmac_token`; `test_erasure_resolution_is_tenant_scoped` | Partial. Erasure requests carry raw subject input only inside the request object and expose `subjectHash` as a secret HMAC token with `subjectTokenKeyId` plus `***PROTECTED***` evidence, so common values such as email addresses are not exposed as plain SHA-256 dictionary targets. Subject resolution matches candidate records only inside the request tenant, so the same subject value in another tenant is ignored. Durable request storage/API, secret rotation registry, and ontology-driven subject graph resolution remain future work. |

SearchAdapter delete/rebuild integration remains future work. |

The security module now defines raw-subject-free executor receipts and certificate evidence, and refuses to certify an erasure manifest unless every ready/deferred action has a matching applied/deferred receipt. SDK-generated TypeScript and browser outputs now create request ids, send tenant/user/role context headers, normalize API errors into a typed object with status/code/message/details/request id/retryability, classify stale-version and permission-denied errors, expose an Ontology-style object/action/media OSDK facade with TypeScript ObjectSet property-keyed `where(...)`, chainable `orderBy(...)`, `$pageSize`/`$pageToken` aliases, raw filter compatibility, and fail-fast invalid property/operator/order evidence, expose SDK package manifest/fingerprint and fail-closed regeneration assertion, expose large ontology object/action registry lookup/live-catalog search/action grouping/dynamic-only drift hints, resolve session bearer tokens through a token provider, retry retryable errors with bounded backoff, poll long-running operations with bounded terminal-state detection, group admin capability rows into browser-action/worker/CLI/runbook/future screen models with backend-supplied execution surface, browser-start, approval, checklist, and blocking reason fields, expose `client.operations.admin.taskPlan(...)`, `adminTaskView(...)`, and `adminTaskPlanScreen(...)` for screen-ready admin tasks, consume server-sent operation events with auth/context/request-id headers, collect cursor pages, and collapse duplicate in-flight action calls by key. The React SDK entrypoint now also exposes a live ontology catalog workspace model for object/action/link grouping, dynamic-only SDK-regeneration hints, and the same ontology index search/action grouping model, a cursor-backed list screen-state hook with accumulated pages/items, `loadMore()`, `reload()`, `reset()`, request id, retryability, and `nextCursor` state, a start-and-poll long-running job hook with `phase`, `status`, `runId`, timestamps, request id, retryability, and terminal success/failure state, an operation event stream hook with `events`, `latestEvent`, request id, retryability, `start()`, `stop()`, and `reset()`, plus admin console launch-action, task-plan, and operations-board models that separate browser-safe actions from operator command/runbook rows, future surfaces, and required backend surfaces. The frontend surface matrix locks all 28 `SDK_CLIENT_SURFACE.helpers` rows to exported TypeScript helper functions and helper-contract proof tests, marks every frontend route that requires `Idempotency-Key` with `requiresIdempotencyKey`, and keeps screen-level SDK recipes present for the main frontend build paths. Generated SDK methods for those surfaces require a caller-supplied idempotency key and fail before network I/O when the key is missing, so retry wrappers cannot accidentally issue each attempt as a new mutation intent. Generated package and browser SDK outputs now decorate fetched object instances with non-enumerable `$link` and `$actions` handles. The request contract proves `order.$link.customer.fetchPage(...)` maps the generated alias to the `OrderCustomer` link API, reads the linked target object through the generic object read boundary, and returns decorated target objects. It also proves `order.$actions.approveOrder.validateAction(params)` sends the source object id/version and typed params to the read-only action validation boundary without Idempotency-Key, while `order.$actions.approveOrder.applyAction(params, { idempotencyKey, onCacheRefresh })` sends the source object id, expected object version, caller-provided idempotency key, and typed params, then surfaces cache-refresh hints from the action edit response. Missing bound-action idempotency still fails before network I/O. The mirror compiles supported filter/order/aggregate syntax to the existing object query payload, fails closed for unknown static properties, unsupported operators, unknown action params, and missing idempotency keys, and the proof test verifies masking, permission, link traversal, aggregate groupBy, action validation, action edit/cache-refresh hints, and action idempotency still run through existing object/action service boundaries.
